| RPR6-15 | typed replay configuration | explicit modes and resource bounds | runtime protection |
| RPR6-16 | tests | A8 suite | none |
| RPR6-17 | docs | A10 final docs | none |

## 5. Method contract

| ID | Area | Required behavior |
|---|---|---|
| M01 | worker start | qualifier replaces the later pending-evidence write guard; it runs before any Replay business write. |
| M02 | timeline/snapshot | require `closed=1` and exact symbol/TF/open/close identity; mismatch refuses before staging. |
| M03 | context | select stored as-of evidence, historical recomputation, or refusal per dependency; never fallback to future data. |
| M04 | staging | no skip semantics. Any mid-run invariant/error discards staged artifacts and records only terminal run failure. |
| M05 | profile port | wallet/account/HTTP access in Replay throws named forbidden-dependency failure before publication. |
| M06 | order | price then signal path: advisory stop, TP without extension, label-free lock, rebase/floor, SELL/add suppression, progressive add. Simulation records price assumptions. |
| M07 | virtual budget | virtual daily-check exception ends `FAILED_PROFILE_EVALUATION`; never consults Production account data. |
| M08 | scope | clear Replay and `RegimeStateService` state in `finally` for success, cancel and error. |

## 6. Database/configuration/logging

| ID | Item | Requirement |
|---|---|---|
| D01 | migration | allocate next free migration only after read-only Flyway-history preflight; never edit V103/V106Г?"V109. |
| D02 | MySQL validation | prove baseline/upgrade; V103 collector dependency must be explicit, never silently satisfied. |
| C01 | `replay.default-mode` | `PRODUCTION_LOGIC_SIMULATION`. |
| C02 | `replay.max-concurrent-runs` | `1`. |
| C03 | `replay.profile.required` | `true`; no wallet-settings fallback. |
| C04 | executor/pool limit | bounded; reject before harming Production freshness. |
| L01 | INFO `REPLAY_QUALIFIED` | run/mode/baseline/config/evidence counts. |
| L02 | INFO `REPLAY_REFUSED` | run/requested mode/reason codes. |
| L03 | DEBUG `REPLAY_DECISION` | run/key/route/result/evidence IDs/order/clocks. |
| L04 | WARN `REPLAY_FORBIDDEN_DEPENDENCY` | component/dependency type/run, no secret. |
| L05 | INFO `REPLAY_COMPLETED` | earned mode/rows/duration/resources. |

## 7. Trading behavior

Production policy is unchanged. ReplayГ?Ts entry/exit/add/TP/lock/stop behavior
changes only by calling the same extracted pure policies with virtual account
data. Fees/slippage are profile data and zero-fee remains explicitly labelled
when selected. Historical-only/skipped work is reproduced from durable outcome
evidence or analysis parity is refused. Same-boundary higher-timeframe
visibility requires recorded/inferred ordering; otherwise strict analysis claim
is refused.

## 8. Tests and validation

| ID | Scenario | Expected result |
|---|---|---|
| X01 | open/identity-mismatched candle | refusal before output |
| X02 | missing context/config/lineage | analysis claim refused; simulation only with declared inputs |
| X03 | excluded price event status | no false evidence failure |
| X04 | account or HTTP port | fail before publication |
| X05 | Replay signal key | stable and never equal to Production ID; near-TP/consumption works |
| X06 | Production policy fixtures | exact ordered stop/TP/lock/floor/SELL/add outcomes |
| X07 | virtual budget exception | named failed run, no account read |
| X08 | experimental observer | baseline never invokes; output labelled experimental |
| X09 | cancellation/error | state cleanup and staged artifact discard |
| X10 | concurrent Replay/load | one-run/pool bounds preserve freshness |
| X11 | real MySQL migration | next migration, B105/V103 policy and upgrade path proven |
| X12 | baseline hash | CI rejects mismatched source/config request |

## 9. Risks and rollback

Risks: policy drift through shared extraction, latency, look-ahead, Flyway
collision and false labels. Mitigate with pure ports, Production regression and
transaction tests, qualification refusal, preflight and mode metrics. Rollback
disables the new Replay route; it never falls back to wallet reads. Schema is
additive and corrected only forward.

## 10. Documentation and reconciliation

After approved implementation and validation only, update relevant
`codex-docs` and `claude-docs` architecture/changelog/trading/investigation
documents. Shared knowledge/decisions change only through dual agreement.
Implementation report must map every RPR6/M/D/C/L/X item and every actual file
to this registry; any missing or unregistered material work rejects acceptance.

the required PDF artifact, and explicitly approved by the user.

# Replay / Production Business-Logic-Parity Redesign

**Revision:** RPR-0.4-DRAFT  
**Status:** DRAFT Г?" NOT APPROVED Г?" IMPLEMENTATION FORBIDDEN  
**Created:** 2026-10-10  
**Scope owner:** Codex implementation / Claude independent review

## 1. Business objective

Make manual Replay reproduce the current paper-trading Production business flow
as closely as durable historical evidence permits, while remaining completely
isolated from Production trading state and wallet data.

The exact intended flow is:

```text
historical candle + historical context + canonical accepted price events
  -> shared Production indicator/signal/decision policies
  -> replay-local virtual wallet/account state
  -> shared sizing/allocation/entry revalidation policies
  -> replay-local position lifecycle
  -> shared TP/SL/profit-lock/continuation/exit policies
  -> replay output + explicit parity certificate
```

Replay must never write `wallet_*`, `paper_position`, `trade_signal`,
`signal_processing_work`, or any other Production trading table.

The result is not a claim that historical Java scheduling, MySQL lock timing,
network delay, external Binance availability, or the historical Production
wallet balance can be reproduced. It is a verified **business-logic parity**
claim only when all required market/decision evidence exists. Otherwise Replay
remains available as a labelled simulation and may not claim parity.

## 2. Confirmed root cause and evidence

The 2026-10-10 source audit established the following:

1. Signal, entry, normal-exit, continuation, profit-lock, and near-TP policy
   use several shared Production services.
2. `ShadowProductionReplayService` separately owns replay wallet cash,
   positions, daily budget, persistence, price-loop lifecycle, and result
   labels. It starts at `INITIAL_CAPITAL=10000`, runs one symbol/position, and
   does not reproduce Production wallet/global controls.
3. A run is labelled `EXACT_PRICE_REPLAY` when at least one accepted price
   event exists, rather than proving coverage for every interval while a
   position was open.
4. Historical derivatives information is not persisted;
   `DerivativesPositioningService.evaluateHistorical()` returns UNAVAILABLE.
   Historical order-book evaluation can likewise be unavailable when snapshots
   were not retained.
5. Replay-only FIX-11K and FIX-11Q observers, and the pre-FIX-122 UI revision,
   have no current Production equivalent and must not contaminate parity runs.
6. Current Replay reads `WalletSettingsRepository` directly and its near-TP
   path can call `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy`, which can request a current
   live Binance minimum notional. Both violate replay account/external-input
   isolation.

## 3. Boundaries

### In scope

- Replay input/evidence qualification and run-level parity status.
- Replay-local virtual wallet state, wallet controls, positions, executions,
  daily statistics, and global limit reproduction.
- Canonical price-event coverage qualification and event ordering.
- Historical context-evidence qualification for derivatives and order book.
- Removal of approved retired Replay-only business/experiment behaviour.
- Parity certificate, diagnostics, migrations, tests, documentation.

### Out of scope

- Any Production strategy change, sizing change, exit change, or wallet change.
- Live-exchange orders, fills, fees, slippage, or order-book execution modelling.
- Replaying database locks, thread scheduling, JVM pauses, WebSocket transport,
  external REST failures, or transaction retries as historical facts.
- FIX-153, FIX-154, and FIX-155 trading-policy changes.
- Deleting Production evidence or existing historical Replay/archive rows.

## 4. Contractual modes

| Mode | Purpose | Missing evidence behaviour | Claim allowed |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` | Reproduce Production rules using an explicitly selected virtual Replay account and complete market/decision evidence. | Refuse the run before execution. | Business-logic parity. |
| `SIMULATION` | Manual what-if/research run. | Continue but persist all approximation reasons. | Simulation only. |
| `EXPERIMENTAL_RESEARCH` | Explicit, separately approved research such as FIX-155. | Per experiment contract. | Never Production parity. |

The default Administration Replay action must be `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY`; the UI
must make `SIMULATION` and `EXPERIMENTAL_RESEARCH` explicit opt-in choices.

## 5. Business-logic-parity eligibility

A `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` run must fail before generated signals or shadow wallet
state are written unless all of the following hold:

1. Every requested candle identity is closed and within the quality contract.
2. A complete immutable `ReplayAccountProfile` is supplied: starting virtual
   USDT, reserve, daily budget, daily loss state, maximum-new-position/global
   limits, and wallet settings. It is copied to replay-only tables at run start
   and is never read from or written to a Production wallet table.
3. The selected replay universe is declared. The virtual account applies
   global limits across every symbol included in that run; no claim is made
   that it reproduces the historical Production wallet's other positions.
4. The selected profile contains versioned execution constraints for relevant
   symbols, including minimum executable notional. Replay never fetches a
   current exchange constraint while evaluating historical events.
5. Every canonical direct price observation required from the first replay
   position open through its terminal close is represented by accepted
   `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` evidence, with no unresolved `PENDING` row in the whole
   consumed range.
6. Required historical order-book evidence exists whenever the feature was
   enabled for the corresponding Production decision.
7. Required historical derivatives evidence exists whenever derivatives were
   enabled and could change confidence or entry authority. Until durable
   derivatives persistence exists, affected requests must be refused in this
   mode rather than silently treating UNAVAILABLE as equivalent.
8. The exact Production configuration/version fingerprint is available for the
   requested period, or all version-dependent decision paths are marked
   unavailable and the mode is refused.

`SIMULATION` may use configured defaults or fixed starting capital but must
store each deviation in its output. It may never be displayed as parity.

### 5.1 Wallet isolation boundary

Replay never reads Production `wallet_asset`, `wallet_settings`,
`wallet_trade`, `wallet_managed_position`, `paper_position`, daily-statistics,
or wallet-snapshot rows as a decision input. The existing data does not retain
complete historical wallet state and is deliberately irrelevant to a Replay
account. A user-selected/versioned `ReplayAccountProfile` is the only account
input. This preserves safe isolation while exercising the same pure sizing and
position-management formulas.

The profile also contains symbol execution constraints used by shared
paper-order guards. `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy` must be split into a pure
minimum-notional evaluator plus a Production-only exchange-constraint provider;
Replay receives immutable profile values and may not issue Binance HTTP.

## 6. Agreed behaviour changes

### 6.1 Replay starting state

Replace hard-coded starting capital with an immutable `ReplayStartingState`.
It is seeded from the selected, versioned `ReplayAccountProfile` and stored
under the replay run ID before processing begins. Shadow wallet/position
services consume only this replay-local state and replay tables.

The run-start state must include profile ID/version, creation timestamp, version/config fingerprint,
virtual USDT/asset balances, daily-budget state, daily loss state, virtual
managed positions, virtual paper positions, replay idempotency keys, and a
versioned wallet/settings/configuration fingerprint. It may not be refreshed
from Production while a run is executing.

### 6.2 Shared paper-wallet business authority

Extract or complete pure shared policies for:

- initial and progressive sizing;
- reserve, daily budget, daily-loss, maximum-new-position, and global
  maximum-open-position controls;
- buy/sell/partial-harvest quantity and cost-basis calculations;
- position average entry, target/stop updates, profit-lock state, and near-TP
  state transitions;
- idempotency-key interpretation.

Production retains its current transactional repositories. Replay receives
replay-local adapters implementing the same policy inputs/outputs. No policy
may branch on `replay=true` to produce a different business decision.

### 6.3 Price evidence

Replace the boolean/label derived from Г?oany event existsГ?? with a coverage
result containing requested interval, covered interval, gaps, accepted event
count, rejected/unresolved count, and every position-open interval requiring
protection.

`BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` rejects incomplete coverage. `SIMULATION` may use
signal-price fallback only with the persisted reason `PRICE_PATH_INCOMPLETE`.

### 6.4 Historical decision evidence

Persist versioned derivatives and order-book observations required by
Production, or mark affected historical intervals ineligible for business-logic
parity. Replay must use the same normalized evaluator and only evidence at or
before the replay clock. It must never call live Binance endpoints.

### 6.5 Event ordering and time

Use canonical UTC `observed_at` and event IDs for price ordering. Define a
single deterministic tie order matching ProductionГ?Ts committed sequence:

1. accepted price protection event;
2. resulting position/wallet state;
3. closed-candle analysis signal;
4. signal-driven position action.

If durable evidence cannot establish the required tie order, business-logic parity
must refuse the run. Replay timestamps remain presentation-neutral UTC;
KSA conversion belongs only to UI/reporting.

### 6.6 Retired Replay-only behaviour

The following are **candidates for removal**, subject to complete-reference
review and approval. They must be removed from baseline parity runs now; any
future research requires an independently approved module and run mode.

| Registry ID | Candidate | Proposed disposition |
|---|---|---|
| RPR-RM-01 | `DefensiveRiskReductionReplayObserver` / FIX-11K | Remove source, controller/UI/output tables only after archive/export compatibility is proved. |
| RPR-RM-02 | `OneCandleContinuationGraceReplayObserver` / FIX-11Q | Remove source, controller/UI/output tables only after archive/export compatibility is proved. |
| RPR-RM-03 | pre-FIX-122 replay revision selector | Remove the UI/API option and old comparison path; Production-current FIX-122 remains the only parity rule. |
| RPR-RM-04 | `EXPERIMENTAL` logic mode if no approved user-facing experiment consumes it | Remove after exact reference search; otherwise retain as an isolated framework with no baseline access. |

`ReplayDataSource.DATABASE` versus `DATASET` is not retired business logic:
both must remain only if a test proves equal outputs. It is a data-access
implementation choice, not an old Production-rule selector.

## 7. Implementation change registry

No implementation item is authorized until this exact revision is approved.

| ID | Area | Required change |
|---|---|---|
| RPR-01 | run contract | Add persisted mode, evidence status, account profile/state ID, version/config fingerprint, and refusal reason to the replay run. |
| RPR-02 | replay account schema/service | Add `ReplayAccountProfile` and immutable run-start-state tables. Seed virtual state only from the selected Replay profile. |
| RPR-03 | wallet authority | Extract/reuse pure paper-wallet policy and provide Production/replay persistence adapters; remove direct Replay reads of `WalletSettingsRepository`. |
| RPR-03a | execution constraints | Split minimum-notional evaluation from live exchange constraint acquisition; inject immutable profile constraints into Replay and prohibit Replay Binance HTTP. |
| RPR-04 | shadow replay | Replace fixed capital/local single-position assumptions with replay-local state and multi-symbol/global-limit coordinator. |
| RPR-05 | price evidence | Add interval coverage validator and replace the current any-event exact-price label. |
| RPR-06 | historical context | Add versioned persistence/lookup/qualification for order book and derivatives, or explicit parity refusal. |
| RPR-07 | timing/order | Persist/use deterministic committed event ordering and reject unknown ordering for parity runs. |
| RPR-08 | certificate | Add immutable parity certificate containing field comparisons, evidence counts, deviations, and final claim. |
| RPR-09 | removals | Execute only the approved RPR-RM entries, including migration/archive compatibility. |
| RPR-10 | UI/API | Make mode explicit; default to business-logic parity; display refusal/deviation/certificate details. |
| RPR-11 | tests | Add unit, integration, MySQL, complete scenario, no-look-ahead, and Production/Replay comparison tests below. |
| RPR-12 | docs | Update architecture, rules, changelog, investigation, operational runbook, and parity contract. |

## 8. Error handling, concurrency, and transactions

- Replay-state creation is isolated and must be consistent for one profile version.
- Business-logic-parity evidence validation happens before replay execution; no
  partial shadow run may be labelled a parity run.
- Replay output writes are transactionally isolated by `test_run_id`; a failed
  run records its failure and certificate status without changing Production.
- Replay must not lock Production wallet/position rows for business mutation.
- Replay-profile/state reads must not expose credentials and must log only IDs,
  UTC times, symbol counts, mode, and evidence result.
- Cancellation preserves isolated run evidence and cannot leave a completed
  parity certificate.

## 9. Logging and observability registry

| ID | Event | Mandatory fields |
|---|---|---|
| RPR-L-01 | virtual starting state created/refused | runId, mode, profile ID/version, virtual balances/position counts, config fingerprint, reason |
| RPR-L-02 | evidence qualified/refused | runId, symbol(s), from/to UTC, candle/price/orderbook/derivatives status, gap counts |
| RPR-L-03 | shadow execution decision | runId, symbol, signal/candle ID, decision code, shared-policy source, replay state ID |
| RPR-L-04 | parity mismatch | runId, field, Production value, replay value, evidence reference, severity |
| RPR-L-05 | certificate completed | runId, claim, matched/mismatched field counts, simulation deviations, duration |
| RPR-L-06 | retired path blocked | runId, removed/experimental feature ID, requested source, outcome |

## 10. Database/configuration/trading impact

- **Database:** additive replay account/snapshot/certificate/evidence schema and any
  required durable historical derivatives observations. Applied migrations are
  never edited. Archive migration/retention is mandatory before deletion of
  retired Replay-only output tables.
- **Configuration:** add replay mode/evidence policy only. Production defaults
  and trading configuration remain unchanged.
- **Trading behaviour:** NONE. Production is read-only to this feature and no
  live/paper Production decision, wallet mutation, position, or protection
  rule may change.
- **Replay behaviour:** baseline becomes evidence-qualified business-logic parity
  or explicitly labelled simulation; retired experimental paths are removed or
  isolated only as approved above.

## 11. Test and validation registry

| ID | Required proof |
|---|---|
| RPR-T-01 | The same profile inputs produce equal Production/replay sizing, reserve, daily budget, daily-loss, and global-position-limit decisions through shared policies. |
| RPR-T-01a | Replay refuses `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` when no immutable replay account profile is selected; it must never silently use a Production wallet row. |
| RPR-T-02 | A missing, unresolved, rejected, or coverage-gapped price event refuses business-logic parity and labels simulation correctly. |
| RPR-T-03 | Price-event/signal same-time ordering matches Production for TP, SL, profit lock, normal exit, and signal SELL. |
| RPR-T-04 | End-to-end initial BUY, progressive add, target extension, rebase, stop, profit lock, near-TP partial harvest, terminal exit, and P&L lifecycle match field-for-field. |
| RPR-T-05 | Historical order book/derivatives use only as-of persisted data; missing evidence refuses parity; no live HTTP occurs. |
| RPR-T-05a | Replay never reads a Production wallet settings row or calls a Binance minimum-notional endpoint; profile values drive the same pure minimum-order decision. |
| RPR-T-06 | Multi-symbol global wallet/max-position test proves no isolated-symbol bypass. |
| RPR-T-07 | Cancellation/error tests prove Production tables remain unchanged and certificates cannot falsely pass. |
| RPR-T-08 | MySQL migration and transaction tests use a disposable database. |
| RPR-T-09 | Dataset and database replay inputs produce equivalent output for the same qualified evidence, or one path is removed. |
| RPR-T-10 | Removal tests prove baseline Replay cannot invoke FIX-11K, FIX-11Q, or pre-FIX-122 paths. |
| RPR-T-11 | Real historical parity suite compares selected known Production windows and produces stored certificates. |

## 12. Risks, rollback, and acceptance

- Risk: historical data required for verified parity may not exist. Mitigation:
  refuse parity and use clearly labelled simulation; do not invent evidence.
- Risk: snapshot schema increases storage. Mitigation: bounded retention and
  archive strategy, never deletion of active/investigation evidence.
- Risk: retiring observers breaks historical UI/archive reads. Mitigation:
  migration and archive/export compatibility tests before removal.
- Rollback: disable new business-logic-parity mode and retain existing archived
  output; Production requires no rollback because it is never mutated.

Acceptance requires: all registry IDs reconciled; no unregistered code change;
all test evidence green; disposable MySQL validation; complete source review by
Claude; Codex verification of the exact reviewed revision; and explicit user
authorization after both agents approve this same document.

5:**Baseline:** `0cb6a9a6cc64ba8ac9d33797bcc30af534fd46e3`; the complete dirty-worktree snapshot must be re-hashed and approved immediately before implementation.  
6:**Supersedes:** RPR-0.4/RPR-0.5 for review only.
7:
8:## 1. Business objective and flow
9:
10:Replay must use current Production paper-trading policies with an isolated
11:virtual account. It must never read Production account state, call HTTP, or
12:call candle-only output historical parity.
13:
14:1. User selects one symbol, virtual profile, window and mode.
15:2. Qualifier validates every input before a generated Replay artifact exists.
16:3. A refusal writes only a run/certificate diagnostic.
17:4. Qualified analysis uses closed, identity-matching candles.
18:5. Simulation invokes pure Production policies with only virtual adapters.
19:6. Results stage privately and publish only on terminal success.
20:
21:Release 1 labels outcomes only `ANALYSIS_PARITY`,
22:`PRODUCTION_LOGIC_SIMULATION`, or `REFUSED_INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE`.
23:`ENTRY_DECISION_PARITY` and historical wallet-lifecycle parity are out of scope:
24:they require account-state evidence that Replay is forbidden to read.
25:
26:## 2. Root cause, evidence and scope
27:
28:Replay currently can include open candles, has null IDs, reads
29:`wallet_settings`, and differs from Production price/signal management.
30:Evidence is current source in `RegressionTestWorker`,
31:`ShadowProductionReplayService`, `ExecutionReplayScope`,
32:`TradeExecutionValidationService`, `DynamicProfitLockService`,
33:`LivePositionProtectionService`, `PositionManagementService`, and
34:`PaperTradingService`.
35:
36:Close-message lineage, deployed JAR/config identity and historical
37:order-book/derivative/sentiment availability are not durable for all old
38:windows. Strict claims are therefore unreachable for such eras unless a future
39:approved forward-capture design is implemented.
40:
41:In scope: single-symbol virtual simulation; qualifier/refusal; closed-candle
42:identity; virtual settings/constraints; stable Replay IDs; pure policy ports;
43:staging; resource limits; tests. Out: Production strategy change, real exchange,
44:Production account reads, multi-symbol scheduling, and retirement of
45:FIX-11K/FIX-11Q/pre-FIX122/EXPERIMENTAL. Those paths remain explicit
46:`EXPERIMENTAL` and cannot enter baseline output.
47:
48:## 3. Read/write boundary
49:
50:| Class | Data |
51:|---|---|
52:| Forbidden Production account state | `wallet_asset`, `wallet_trade`, `wallet_managed_position`, `paper_position`, `wallet_settings`, account snapshots and daily account statistics. |
53:| Permitted read-only evidence | `candle`, `trade_signal`, `market_price_event`, `signal_processing_work`, `candle_analysis_outcome`, `order_book_snapshot`, `fix122_evaluation`, `buy_htf_assessment_audit`, `execution_opportunity`, subject to qualifier as-of rules. |
54:| Replay-owned writes | `regression_test_run`, `analysis_test_signal`, `wallet_execution_test`, `wallet_position_test`, and new RPR6 profile/certificate/staging objects. |
55:
56:`NON_CANONICAL` and `PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED` observations are deliberate
57:exclusions. Required accepted observations are `LEGACY`/`APPLIED`, retaining id
58:and ordering slot.
59:

82:## 5. Method contract
83:
84:| ID | Area | Required behavior |
85:|---|---|---|
86:| M01 | worker start | qualifier replaces the later pending-evidence write guard; it runs before any Replay business write. |
87:| M02 | timeline/snapshot | require `closed=1` and exact symbol/TF/open/close identity; mismatch refuses before staging. |
88:| M03 | context | select stored as-of evidence, historical recomputation, or refusal per dependency; never fallback to future data. |
89:| M04 | staging | no skip semantics. Any mid-run invariant/error discards staged artifacts and records only terminal run failure. |
90:| M05 | profile port | wallet/account/HTTP access in Replay throws named forbidden-dependency failure before publication. |
91:| M06 | order | price then signal path: advisory stop, TP without extension, label-free lock, rebase/floor, SELL/add suppression, progressive add. Simulation records price assumptions. |
92:| M07 | virtual budget | virtual daily-check exception ends `FAILED_PROFILE_EVALUATION`; never consults Production account data. |
93:| M08 | scope | clear Replay and `RegimeStateService` state in `finally` for success, cancel and error. |

111:## 7. Trading behavior
112:
113:Production policy is unchanged. Replayƒ?Ts entry/exit/add/TP/lock/stop behavior
114:changes only by calling the same extracted pure policies with virtual account
115:data. Fees/slippage are profile data and zero-fee remains explicitly labelled
116:when selected. Historical-only/skipped work is reproduced from durable outcome
117:evidence or analysis parity is refused. Same-boundary higher-timeframe
118:visibility requires recorded/inferred ordering; otherwise strict analysis claim
119:is refused.

122:
123:| ID | Scenario | Expected result |
124:|---|---|---|
125:| X01 | open/identity-mismatched candle | refusal before output |
126:| X02 | missing context/config/lineage | analysis claim refused; simulation only with declared inputs |
127:| X03 | excluded price event status | no false evidence failure |
128:| X04 | account or HTTP port | fail before publication |
129:| X05 | Replay signal key | stable and never equal to Production ID; near-TP/consumption works |
130:| X06 | Production policy fixtures | exact ordered stop/TP/lock/floor/SELL/add outcomes |
131:| X07 | virtual budget exception | named failed run, no account read |
132:| X08 | experimental observer | baseline never invokes; output labelled experimental |
133:| X09 | cancellation/error | state cleanup and staged artifact discard |
134:| X10 | concurrent Replay/load | one-run/pool bounds preserve freshness |
135:| X11 | real MySQL migration | next migration, B105/V103 policy and upgrade path proven |
136:| X12 | baseline hash | CI rejects mismatched source/config request |
137:
138:## 9. Risks and rollback
139:
140:Risks: policy drift through shared extraction, latency, look-ahead, Flyway
141:collision and false labels. Mitigate with pure ports, Production regression and
142:transaction tests, qualification refusal, preflight and mode metrics. Rollback
143:disables the new Replay route; it never falls back to wallet reads. Schema is
144:additive and corrected only forward.

1:# FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY rule ƒ?" 2026-10-10
2:
3:- `data_quality_valid=true` is required before a bearish 1h signal can be
4:  treated as trusted. `NULL` is unknown and fails closed for bearish evidence.
5:- A trusted `STRONG_SELL` at `confidence_score >= 70` blocks the BUY.
6:- A trusted `SELL`, or trusted `STRONG_SELL` below 70, is a SOFT conflict:
7:  profiles receive effective 1h `NEUTRAL`, and any allowed initial BUY is
8:  capped at 25%.
9:- A bearish 5m still returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO` before any 1h result.
10:- Existing progressive/add rules retain their raw 1h requirement; a SOFT 1h
11:  never authorizes an add.
12:
13:# FIX-153 candle continuity rule ƒ?" 2026-10-10
14:
15:- Missing price evidence never becomes invented OHLCV data and never bypasses
16:  the data-quality gate.
17:- Automatic reconciliation may restore only one exact, final Binance candle.
18:  Its recovered/backfilled row has no close token and cannot directly cause
19:  analysis, a signal, or a trade.
20:- A later normal live close is the only route that resumes ordinary signal
21:  generation after a repaired quality window.
1:# Trade Investigation Agent
2:
3:## Role
4:
5:Investigate one Crypto AI Trader trade from durable evidence. Explain what
6:happened, why the system entered and exited, whether it followed the intended
7:rules, and whether the entry/exit was supported by the information available
8:at that time.
9:
10:Investigations are read-only. Do not change code, configuration, services,
11:database records, review states, Replay work, or deployment state.
12:
13:## Required input
14:
15:Obtain or resolve:
16:
17:- Environment and application: local, test, or production.
18:- Database host, port, and schema.
19:- One trade reference: `paper_position.id`, `wallet_trade.id`, or a symbol plus
20:  a bounded approximate entry-time window.
21:- Optional question or suspected issue.
22:
23:Before analysis, confirm the symbol, trade/position ID, running version or FIX
24:level, and ingestion mode. Read the actual schema before choosing tables or
25:columns. Do not assume a table is in `crypto_ai` when it may belong to
26:`crypto_ai_v2`.
27:
28:## Evidence rules
29:
30:- Use configured database and SSH/log connections without displaying secrets.
31:- Use bounded, read-only queries and targeted log searches.
32:- Treat stored timestamps as UTC; display both UTC and KSA (`UTC+03:00`).
33:- Attach record IDs and timestamped log evidence to every factual conclusion.
34:- Distinguish **confirmed fact**, **supported inference**, and **missing or
35:  conflicting evidence**.
36:- Missing logs or records do not prove that no execution/effect occurred.
37:- A saved signal does not prove execution. A callback return does not prove a
38:  transaction committed. Under FIX-153, `market_price_event.APPLIED` proves a
39:  committed successful protection callback, but can be a no-op when there was
40:  no open position; find the position/trade mutation separately.
41:- Current configuration does not prove which settings were active at entry. If
42:  no configuration history exists, report that limitation.
43:
44:## Investigation workflow
45:
46:### 1. Resolve identity and environment
47:
48:Link the trade reference to, where available:
49:
50:- `paper_position`
51:- `wallet_trade`
52:- entry and exit `trade_signal`
53:- `signal_processing_work`
54:- wallet/position execution records
55:
56:Record entry and exit times in UTC and KSA. Confirm whether the trader ran in
57:local-candle OFF mode or a legacy shared-market mode.
58:
59:### 2. Collect a small evidence bundle
60:
61:Start with exact IDs and a narrow time window. Expand only when the evidence
62:requires it. Collect:
63:
64:| Evidence | Establishes |
65:| --- | --- |
66:| `paper_position` | lifecycle, entry/exit, stop, target, status |
67:| `wallet_trade` | executed quantity, prices, fees, linked signal |
68:| `trade_signal` | decision, strategy, scores, entry eligibility, reasons |
69:| `signal_processing_work` | claim, attempt, completion, expiry, uncertainty |
70:| `candle` / `technical_indicator` | market data and analysis inputs available then |
71:| `market_price_event` | intra-candle protection observations and outcomes |
72:| configuration/version evidence | applicable rules, when historically available |
73:| application and timing logs | analysis, execution, protection, exceptions, waits |
74:
75:Avoid fetching large signal explanation fields until they are needed.
76:
77:### 3. Reconstruct the timeline
78:
79:Build a timeline, explicitly showing missing stages:
80:
81:`candle closed ƒ+' signal saved ƒ+' work claimed ƒ+' locks acquired ƒ+' entry committed ƒ+' protection observations ƒ+' exit decision ƒ+' exit committed`
82:
83:Measure separately:
84:
85:- candle close to signal generation;
86:- signal generation to processing/entry;
87:- observation time to protection completion;
88:- exit-trigger evidence to recorded exit.
89:
90:Correlate delays only with logs/events for the same symbol, position, signal,
91:or price-event identity. A nearby slow SQL query is not proof of causation.
92:
93:### 4. Assess entry correctness and quality
94:
95:Use only evidence that was available at entry. Check:
96:
97:- signal mode, closed-candle identity, freshness, and data quality;
98:- final decision and entry-allowed result;
99:- BTC context, higher-timeframe, ATR, liquidity, risk/reward, sizing, and
100:  strategy gates that applied at that time;
101:- correct signal/position linkage and absence of duplicate/stale-retry entry;
102:- quantity, wallet balance, risk limits, fees, and fill/reference-price gap.
103:
104:Return two separate conclusions:
105:
106:- **Execution correctness:** followed or violated the implemented rules.
107:- **Entry quality:** supported, weak, or unverifiable from contemporaneous
108:  evidence.
109:
110:Subsequent price movement may evaluate the outcome; it must never be presented
111:as information the entry decision had.
112:
113:### 5. Reconstruct protection and stop-loss behavior
114:
115:For FIX-153, inspect accepted `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` price events and retain
116:`PENDING`, `NON_CANONICAL`, and `PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED` records as evidence
117:gaps or exclusions. Never substitute candle OHLC for absent intra-candle price
118:evidence.
119:
120:For each relevant accepted observation:
121:
122:1. Determine the then-active stop, target, trailing stop, and profit-lock state.
123:2. Find the first accepted observation that met an exit threshold.
124:3. Confirm that protection evaluated it and identify the resulting position or
125:   wallet execution record.
126:4. Explain delays, rejections, stale/out-of-order classification, exceptions,
127:   lock waits, or pool pressure.
128:
129:Classify the exit path accurately: fixed stop-loss, trailing stop, profit-lock,
130:take-profit, signal-driven exit, manual exit, or unknown.
131:
132:### 6. Assess exit correctness and quality
133:
134:Compare the recorded exit reason, quantity, and price with trigger evidence.
135:Check threshold reach, accepted-observation identity, protection timing, and
136:the committed execution. Calculate maximum favourable/adverse movement during
137:the holding period separately from the contemporaneous exit assessment.
138:
139:### 7. Reconcile financial results
140:
141:Recalculate using execution records:
142:
143:- bought/sold quantities and weighted entry/exit prices;
144:- gross P&L, fees, and net P&L;
145:- partial exits, remaining quantity, and realized versus unrealized values.
146:
147:Flag discrepancies among wallet records, position records, and UI output.
148:
149:### 8. Diagnose cause and use Replay carefully
150:
151:Classify findings as one or more of:
152:
153:- strategy decision;
154:- data freshness or coverage;
155:- analysis delay;
156:- execution failure;
157:- protection failure;
158:- database, lock, or connection-pool contention;
159:- accounting/UI discrepancy;
160:- insufficient evidence.
161:
162:Run Replay only as a controlled comparison with equivalent candles, accepted
163:prices, strategy version, and settings. State every unavailable input or
164:material difference. Never claim parity by substituting candle OHLC for missing
165:price evidence or treating `PENDING` as accepted.
166:
167:## Required final report
168:
169:1. **Trade identity and environment** ƒ?" IDs, symbol, version/mode, scope.
170:2. **UTC/KSA timeline** ƒ?" evidence-backed sequence and latency breakdown.
171:3. **Entry** ƒ?" execution correctness and entry quality.
172:4. **Exit** ƒ?" execution correctness and exit quality.
173:5. **Stop-loss/profit-lock** ƒ?" worked, failed, or unverifiable, with evidence.
174:6. **P&L reconciliation** ƒ?" quantities, prices, fees, and discrepancies.
175:7. **Root cause and confidence** ƒ?" confirmed defect, valid loss, configuration
176:   issue, delay, or insufficient evidence.
177:8. **Evidence references** ƒ?" record IDs, query/log timestamps, code/config
178:   references.
179:9. **Missing evidence** ƒ?" exactly what prevents a stronger conclusion.
180:10. **Recommended correction and regression checks** ƒ?" only when justified.
181:
182:A profitable trade can violate the rules, and a losing trade can be a correct
183:execution. State those independently.

codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-166-

codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md:169:  database data, or Replay result was changed.
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md:170:- Confirmed shared authority: Replay regenerates technical indicators through
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-171-  `TechnicalIndicatorService`, signals through `AnalysisService`, BUY authority
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-172-  through `ExecutionIntelligenceService`, BUY/SELL validation through
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-173-  `TradeExecutionValidationService`, and normal exit, TP-continuation,
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-174-  profit-lock, and near-TP policies through the corresponding shared policy
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md:175:  classes. When canonical `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` price events exist, the Replay
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-176-  consumes them in timestamp order before making the same close-candle signal
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-177-  visible, matching the documented live ordering.
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md:178:- Confirmed boundary: Replay is a shadow simulation, not a transactionally
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-179-  identical copy of Production. It starts with fixed capital of 10,000 USDT,
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md:180:  manages one symbol/position in isolation, uses replay-local daily budget and
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-181-  position state, and writes only `*_test` tables. It therefore cannot
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-182-  reproduce an actual wallet balance, simultaneous positions, global
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-183-  `maxOpenPositions`, daily loss limit, idempotency races, database locks, or
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-184-  real execution latency.
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-185-- Confirmed historical-evidence limitation: derivatives history is not
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md:186:  persisted, so historical Replay always receives `UNAVAILABLE` derivatives
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-187-  context when that feature is enabled. Historical order-book evaluation uses
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-188-  stored snapshots only and becomes `UNAVAILABLE` when no snapshot exists,
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-189-  whereas live Production samples its in-memory/live Binance source. Such runs
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md:190:  are useful simulations but are not full signal-parity proof unless the
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-191-  required historical evidence exists and is reported.
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md:192:- Confirmed price-evidence limitation: a Replay run labels itself
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md:193:  `EXACT_PRICE_REPLAY` whenever at least one accepted price event exists. It
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-194-  does not prove complete tick coverage for every time an open position needed
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-195-  protection. Windows with no accepted events fall back to signal prices and
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-196-  are labelled `SIGNAL_PRICE_FALLBACK`; neither fallback nor partial coverage
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-197-  should be described as tick-exact reproduction.
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-198-- Test evidence: Java 21 focused tests passed for profit-lock/TP-extension
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md:199:  parity, position exit/continuation, near-TP policy, and shared sizing.
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md:200:  Existing Replay-specific coverage is narrow: it does not execute a complete
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md:201:  Production-versus-Replay scenario across entry, add, wallet controls,
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-202-  price-event ordering, partial harvest, and exit.
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md:203:- Conclusion: Replay is suitable for controlled, manual strategy regression
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-204-  and the approved FIX-155 research work only when each run carries explicit
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-205-  evidence-quality labels. It is not yet valid to claim it is fully identical
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md:206:  to Production in all phases. A future parity program must add deterministic
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md:207:  scenario tests and a run-level eligibility contract before using Replay as a
codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md-208-  release-equivalence certificate.

514:    private int verifyEventResolution(long runId, String symbol, String interval, List<Candle> candles,
515:                                      int progressStart, int progressEnd) {
516:        int resolved = 0;
517:        int size = candles.size();
518:        for (int i = 0; i < size; i++) {
519:            checkStopRequested(runId);
520:            Candle candle = candles.get(i);
521:            List<Candle> latestAtEvent = candleRepository.findClosedCandlesAtOrBefore(
522:                    symbol, interval, candle.getOpenTime(), PageRequest.of(0, 1));
523:            if (!latestAtEvent.isEmpty() && candle.getOpenTime().equals(latestAtEvent.get(0).getOpenTime())) {
524:                resolved++;
525:            }
526:            if (i % 25 == 0 && size > 0) {
527:                int p = progressStart + (int) Math.round((progressEnd - progressStart) * (i / (double) size));
528:                updateRun(runId, "RUNNING", p,
529:                        "Replaying " + interval + " candle " + (i + 1) + "/" + size, null);
530:            }
531:        }
532:        return resolved;
533:    }

535:    private FreshReplayStats generateFreshSignals(
536:            long runId,
537:            String symbol,
538:            Instant executionStart,
539:            Instant executionEnd,
540:            List<Candle> oneMinuteCandles,
541:            List<Candle> fiveMinuteCandles,
542:            List<Candle> oneHourCandles,
543:            String btcSymbol,
544:            List<Candle> btcOneMinuteCandles,
545:            List<Candle> btcFiveMinuteCandles,
546:            List<Candle> btcOneHourCandles,
547:            ReplayDataSource dataSource,
548:            ReplayDataset replayDataset
549:    ) {
550:        List<ReplayCandle> timeline = new java.util.ArrayList<>();
551:        oneMinuteCandles.forEach(c -> timeline.add(new ReplayCandle(symbol, "1m", c, true)));
552:        fiveMinuteCandles.forEach(c -> timeline.add(new ReplayCandle(symbol, "5m", c, true)));
553:        oneHourCandles.forEach(c -> timeline.add(new ReplayCandle(symbol, "1h", c, true)));
554:        btcOneMinuteCandles.forEach(c -> timeline.add(new ReplayCandle(btcSymbol, "1m", c, false)));
555:        btcFiveMinuteCandles.forEach(c -> timeline.add(new ReplayCandle(btcSymbol, "5m", c, false)));
556:        btcOneHourCandles.forEach(c -> timeline.add(new ReplayCandle(btcSymbol, "1h", c, false)));
557:        timeline.sort(java.util.Comparator
558:                .comparing((ReplayCandle rc) -> rc.candle().getOpenTime().plusSeconds(intervalSeconds(rc.interval())))
559:                .thenComparingInt(rc -> intervalOrder(rc.interval()))
560:                .thenComparingInt(rc -> rc.primary() ? 1 : 0));
561:
562:        int oneMinuteSignals = 0, fiveMinuteSignals = 0, oneHourSignals = 0;
563:        int oneMinuteBuys = 0, fiveMinuteBuys = 0, oneHourBuys = 0;
564:        int buys = 0, watches = 0, sells = 0, strongSells = 0, errors = 0;
565:        java.util.List<TradeSignal> generatedSignals = new java.util.ArrayList<>();
566:
567:        // FIX-11L: Replay-only deep profiling for the Generate fresh signals stage.
568:        // These monotonic timers observe existing calls only; they do not alter inputs,
569:        // call ordering, persistence, thresholds, or any Production trading behavior.
570:        ReplayFreshSignalProfiler profiler = new ReplayFreshSignalProfiler(runId, dataSource);
571:        // FIX-11M: Enable aggregate timing only for this Replay worker thread.
572:        // The shared AnalysisService decision path is unchanged; this is observation-only.
573:        analysisService.beginReplayAnalysisProfiling(runId);
574:        try (ExecutionReplayScope.Scope ignored = replayScope.open(runId, List.of(), o -> {})) {
575:        // FIX-043 Replay parity: fresh Replay has always generated technical snapshots/signals
576:        // chronologically for EVERY closed candle in the timeline. Production now restores the
577:        // same no-gap contract with asynchronous close-event processing plus chronological recovery.
578:        // Do not "optimize" Replay by sampling every Nth candle or by copying the production recovery
579:        // scheduler cadence; that would recreate the exact 1m->~5m blind-gap incident FIX-043 fixes.
580:        for (int index = 0; index < timeline.size(); index++) {
581:            checkStopRequested(runId);
582:            ReplayCandle replay = timeline.get(index);
583:            Candle candle = replay.candle();
584:            Instant evaluationTime = candle.getOpenTime().plusSeconds(intervalSeconds(replay.interval()));
585:            boolean inRequestedWindow = !evaluationTime.isBefore(executionStart) && !evaluationTime.isAfter(executionEnd);
586:            try {
587:                long operationStartedNs = System.nanoTime();
588:                java.util.Optional<IndicatorSnapshot> snapshot;
589:                try {
590:                    snapshot = dataSource == ReplayDataSource.DATASET
591:                            ? technicalIndicatorService.calculateSnapshotForRegression(
592:                                    replay.symbol(), replay.interval(), candle.getOpenTime(), replayDataset)
593:                            : technicalIndicatorService.calculateSnapshotForRegression(
594:                                    replay.symbol(), replay.interval(), candle.getOpenTime());
595:                } finally {
596:                    profiler.recordSnapshot(System.nanoTime() - operationStartedNs);
597:                }
598:                if (snapshot.isEmpty()) {
599:                    if (inRequestedWindow) {
600:                        errors++;
601:                        saveGenerationError(runId, replay.symbol(), replay.interval(), candle,
602:                                "Not enough historical candles to calculate the technical snapshot");
603:                    }
604:                    continue;
605:                }
606:
607:                // Do not generate a signal whose candle would only have closed after the
608:                // requested test end. Warm-up rows before executionStart are intentionally
609:                // generated for as-of context, but are not counted or displayed as test output.
610:                if (evaluationTime.isAfter(executionEnd)) continue;
611:
612:                replayScope.reference(evaluationTime);
613:                operationStartedNs = System.nanoTime();
614:                TradeSignal generated;
615:                try {
616:                    generated = analysisService.analyzeForRegression(snapshot.get(), evaluationTime);
617:                } finally {
618:                    profiler.recordAnalysis(System.nanoTime() - operationStartedNs);
619:                }
620:                replayScope.appendSignal(generated);
621:                SignalDecision decision = generated.getDecision();
622:                if (replay.primary()) generatedSignals.add(generated);
623:
624:                if (replay.primary() && inRequestedWindow) {
625:                    if ("1m".equals(replay.interval())) { oneMinuteSignals++; if (isBuy(decision)) oneMinuteBuys++; }
626:                    else if ("5m".equals(replay.interval())) { fiveMinuteSignals++; if (isBuy(decision)) fiveMinuteBuys++; }
627:                    else if ("1h".equals(replay.interval())) { oneHourSignals++; if (isBuy(decision)) oneHourBuys++; }
628:
629:                    if (decision == SignalDecision.BUY || decision == SignalDecision.STRONG_BUY) buys++;
630:                    else if (decision == SignalDecision.WATCH) watches++;
631:                    else if (decision == SignalDecision.SELL) sells++;
632:                    else if (decision == SignalDecision.STRONG_SELL) strongSells++;

649:                            decision == null ? null : decision.name(),

178:    public synchronized long start(RegressionTestRunRequest request, ReplayDataSource dataSource) {
179:        if (request == null || request.symbol() == null || request.symbol().isBlank()
180:                || request.startTime() == null || request.endTime() == null
181:                || !request.endTime().isAfter(request.startTime())) {

189:                """, Integer.class);

1:package com.crypto.execution.service;
2:
3:import com.crypto.domain.TradeSignal;
4:import com.crypto.execution.domain.ExecutionOpportunity;

16:/**
17: * Thread-bound data/persistence override used only by Administration historical replay.
18: *
19: * The execution algorithms remain the production services. While this scope is active,
20: * their signal lookups are resolved exclusively from replay-generated signals as-of the
21: * current replay timestamp, and opportunity persistence is redirected to the supplied
22: * shadow sink. Production repositories are never read/written by the scoped execution path.
23: */
24:@Component
25:public class ExecutionReplayScope {
26:
27:    /** FIX-109: Production parity is the default replay contract. Experimental mode is
28:     * explicit so replay-only candidate rules can never contaminate a parity result. */
29:    public enum ReplayLogicMode { PRODUCTION_PARITY, EXPERIMENTAL }
30:
31:    private final ThreadLocal<State> state = new ThreadLocal<>();
32:
33:    public Scope open(long runId, List<TradeSignal> replaySignals, Consumer<ExecutionOpportunity> opportunitySink) {
34:        return open(runId, replaySignals, opportunitySink, ReplayLogicMode.PRODUCTION_PARITY);
35:    }
36:
37:    public Scope open(long runId, List<TradeSignal> replaySignals, Consumer<ExecutionOpportunity> opportunitySink,
38:                      ReplayLogicMode logicMode) {
39:        if (state.get() != null) throw new IllegalStateException("Execution replay scope is already active on this thread");
40:        List<TradeSignal> immutable = replaySignals == null ? List.of() : replaySignals.stream()
41:                .filter(s -> s != null && s.getGeneratedAt() != null)
42:                .sorted(Comparator.comparing(TradeSignal::getGeneratedAt))
43:                .toList();
44:        state.set(new State(runId, immutable, opportunitySink, logicMode == null ? ReplayLogicMode.PRODUCTION_PARITY : logicMode));
45:        return new Scope();
46:    }
47:
48:    /** FIX-122: run-local executed boundaries, independent of Production ledger/history. */
49:    public void recordStop(String symbol, long positionId, Instant at, String reason) {
50:        if (!"STOP_LOSS".equals(reason)) return;
51:        State s=required();
52:        StopLossEvidencePolicy.Boundary previous=s.stops.get(symbol);
53:        if (previous==null || !at.isBefore(previous.executedAt()))
54:            s.stops.put(symbol,new StopLossEvidencePolicy.Boundary(positionId,at));
55:    }
56:    public StopLossEvidencePolicy.Boundary stopBoundary(String symbol, Instant asOf) {
57:        StopLossEvidencePolicy.Boundary b=required().stops.get(symbol);
58:        return b!=null && !b.executedAt().isAfter(asOf) ? b : null;
59:    }
60:    public Instant referenceInstant() { return required().reference; }
61:    public boolean fix122Enabled() { return required().fix122Enabled; }
62:    public void fix122Enabled(boolean value) { required().fix122Enabled=value; }
63:
64:    public boolean active() { return state.get() != null; }
65:    public long runId() { return required().runId; }
66:    public ReplayLogicMode logicMode() { return required().logicMode; }
67:    public boolean experimental() { return active() && required().logicMode == ReplayLogicMode.EXPERIMENTAL; }
68:    public boolean productionParity() { return active() && required().logicMode == ReplayLogicMode.PRODUCTION_PARITY; }
69:
70:    public void reference(Instant reference) { required().reference = reference; }
71:
72:    /** FIX-056: expose the exact latest replay market-price event to the shared execution-price authority. */
73:    public void referencePrice(String symbol, Instant observedAt, java.math.BigDecimal price) {
74:        State s = required();
75:        s.reference = observedAt;
76:        s.latestPrice = new ReplayPrice(symbol, observedAt, price);
77:    }
78:
79:    public Optional<ReplayPrice> latestPrice() {
80:        State s = required();
81:        return Optional.ofNullable(s.latestPrice);
82:    }
83:
84:    /** FIX-112A: Replay-local execution ledger keyed to the exact triggering signal. */
85:    public boolean entryConsumed(Long signalId) {
86:        return active() && signalId != null && required().consumedSignalIds.contains(signalId);
87:    }
88:
89:    /** FIX-112A: record only after that exact shadow BUY (initial or add) executes. */
90:    public void markEntryConsumed(Long signalId) {
91:        if (signalId != null) required().consumedSignalIds.add(signalId);
92:    }
93:
94:    /**
95:     * Adds a freshly generated replay signal to the as-of context stream. Signal generation
96:     * uses this incrementally so the production confluence/BTC services can resolve only
97:     * context that had genuinely been generated by the current replay timestamp.
98:     */
99:    public void appendSignal(TradeSignal signal) {
100:        if (signal == null || signal.getGeneratedAt() == null) return;
101:        State s = required();
102:        s.signals.add(signal);
103:        s.signals.sort(Comparator.comparing(TradeSignal::getGeneratedAt));
104:    }
105:
106:    public List<TradeSignal> recent(String symbol, String interval, Instant reference, int limit) {
107:        State s = required();
108:        Instant ref = reference != null ? reference : s.reference;
109:        if (ref == null) return List.of();
110:        return s.signals.stream()
111:                .filter(x -> symbol.equals(x.getSymbol()) && interval.equals(x.getInterval()))
112:                .filter(x -> !x.getGeneratedAt().isAfter(ref))
113:                .sorted(Comparator.comparing(TradeSignal::getGeneratedAt).reversed())
114:                .limit(limit)
115:                .toList();
116:    }
117:
118:    public Optional<TradeSignal> latestAtOrBefore(String symbol, String interval, Instant reference) {
119:        return recent(symbol, interval, reference, 1).stream().findFirst();
120:    }
121:
122:    /**
123:     * FIX-141: Replay-side equivalent of
124:     * TradeSignalRepository#findTopBySymbolAndIntervalAndCandleOpenTimeLessThanEqualAndGeneratedAtLessThanEqualOrderByCandleOpenTimeDesc.
125:     * Selects by candle open time (close-time ordering), not generatedAt, so Replay cannot diverge
126:     * from Production on which BTC snapshot it picks. candleOpenTimeBound is the caller's look-ahead
127:     * bound (reference minus the interval duration); generatedAt must also be at or before reference.
128:     */
129:    public Optional<TradeSignal> latestClosedAtOrBefore(String symbol, String interval, Instant reference, Instant candleOpenTimeBound) {
130:        State s = required();
131:        if (reference == null || candleOpenTimeBound == null) return Optional.empty();
132:        return s.signals.stream()
133:                .filter(x -> symbol.equals(x.getSymbol()) && interval.equals(x.getInterval()))
134:                .filter(x -> x.getGeneratedAt() != null && !x.getGeneratedAt().isAfter(reference))
135:                .filter(x -> x.getCandleOpenTime() != null && !x.getCandleOpenTime().isAfter(candleOpenTimeBound))
136:                .max(Comparator.comparing(TradeSignal::getCandleOpenTime));
137:    }
138:
139:    public Optional<TradeSignal> previousBefore(String symbol, String interval, Instant reference) {
140:        State s = required();
141:        return s.signals.stream()
142:                .filter(x -> symbol.equals(x.getSymbol()) && interval.equals(x.getInterval()))
143:                .filter(x -> x.getGeneratedAt().isBefore(reference))
144:                .max(Comparator.comparing(TradeSignal::getGeneratedAt));
145:    }
146:
147:    public Optional<ExecutionOpportunity> currentOpportunity(String symbol, List<String> statuses) {
148:        ExecutionOpportunity o = required().opportunity;
149:        if (o == null || !symbol.equals(o.getSymbol())) return Optional.empty();
150:        if (statuses != null && !statuses.isEmpty() && !statuses.contains(o.getStatus())) return Optional.empty();
151:        return Optional.of(o);
152:    }
153:
154:    public ExecutionOpportunity saveOpportunity(ExecutionOpportunity opportunity) {
155:        State s = required();
156:        s.opportunity = opportunity;
157:        if (s.opportunitySink != null) s.opportunitySink.accept(opportunity);
158:        return opportunity;
159:    }
160:
161:    public final class Scope implements AutoCloseable {
162:        private boolean closed;
163:        @Override public void close() {
164:            if (!closed) {
165:                state.remove();
166:                closed = true;
167:            }
168:        }
169:    }
170:
171:    private State required() {
172:        State s = state.get();
173:        if (s == null) throw new IllegalStateException("No Administration execution replay scope is active");
174:        return s;
175:    }
176:
177:    public record ReplayPrice(String symbol, Instant observedAt, java.math.BigDecimal price) {}
178:
179:    private static final class State {
180:        final long runId;
181:        final List<TradeSignal> signals;
182:        final Consumer<ExecutionOpportunity> opportunitySink;
183:        final ReplayLogicMode logicMode;
184:        boolean fix122Enabled = true;
185:        final java.util.Map<String,StopLossEvidencePolicy.Boundary> stops = new java.util.HashMap<>();
186:        Instant reference;
187:        ExecutionOpportunity opportunity;
188:        ReplayPrice latestPrice;
189:        final Set<Long> consumedSignalIds = new HashSet<>();
190:        State(long runId, List<TradeSignal> signals, Consumer<ExecutionOpportunity> opportunitySink, ReplayLogicMode logicMode) {
191:            this.runId = runId;
192:            this.signals = new ArrayList<>(signals);
193:            this.opportunitySink = opportunitySink;
194:            this.logicMode = logicMode;
195:        }
196:    }
197:}

61:    public ReplayStats replay(long runId, String symbol, Instant executionStart, Instant executionEnd,
62:                              List<TradeSignal> generatedSignals,
63:                              List<MarketPriceEventService.PriceEvent> productionPriceEvents,
64:                              BooleanSupplier stopRequested) {
65:        List<TradeSignal> timeline = generatedSignals.stream()
66:                .filter(s -> s != null && s.getGeneratedAt() != null)
67:                .sorted(Comparator.comparing(TradeSignal::getGeneratedAt)
68:                        .thenComparingInt(s -> intervalOrder(s.getInterval())))
69:                .toList();
70:
71:        TradeSignal latest1m = null;
72:        TradeSignal latest5m = null;
73:        TradeSignal latest1h = null;
74:        ShadowPosition open = null;
75:        BigDecimal cash = INITIAL_CAPITAL;
76:        BigDecimal realized = BigDecimal.ZERO;
77:        int trades = 0, wins = 0, losses = 0;
78:        int consecutiveFinalOneMinuteStrongSells = 0;
79:        WalletSettings replayWalletSettings = walletSettings();
80:        BigDecimal replayDailyBudget = executionSizingPolicy.initialDailyBudget(
81:                cash, replayWalletSettings.getMinimumUsdtReserve(),
82:                replayWalletSettings.getBaseTradeAmountUsdt(), replayWalletSettings.getMaximumDailyNewPositions());
83:        LocalDate replayTradeDate = null;
84:        int replayExecutedNewPositions = 0;
85:        // FIX-052: when Production live-price events exist, Replay uses them as the
86:        // authoritative mechanical-protection clock. Older windows created before V64
87:        // transparently retain candle/signal-close fallback and are therefore not claimed
88:        // as tick-exact parity runs.
89:        List<MarketPriceEventService.PriceEvent> livePrices = productionPriceEvents == null
90:                ? List.of()
91:                : productionPriceEvents.stream()
92:                    .filter(e -> e != null && e.observedAt() != null && e.price() != null)
93:                    .sorted(Comparator.comparing(MarketPriceEventService.PriceEvent::observedAt))
94:                    .toList();
95:        boolean exactPriceReplay = !livePrices.isEmpty();
96:        int livePriceIndex = 0;
97:        // FIX-109: normal Regression/Replay runs are Production-Parity by default.
98:        // Experimental rules require an explicitly opened EXPERIMENTAL scope.
99:        String replayLogicMode = ExecutionReplayScope.ReplayLogicMode.PRODUCTION_PARITY.name();
100:        defensiveRiskReductionObserver.logRunStart(runId, symbol);
101:        oneCandleContinuationGraceReplayObserver.startRun(runId, symbol);
102:
103:        try (ExecutionReplayScope.Scope ignored = replayScope.open(runId, timeline,
104:                opportunity -> {
105:                    TradeSignal latest = opportunity == null ? null : opportunity.getLatestSignal();
106:                    if (latest != null && latest.getGeneratedAt() != null
107:                            && !latest.getGeneratedAt().isBefore(executionStart)
108:                            && !latest.getGeneratedAt().isAfter(executionEnd)) {
109:                        persistProductionOpportunity(runId, opportunity);
110:                    }
111:                })) {
112:        // FIX-122: independent OLD comparison revision; ordinary Replay defaults to NEW.
113:        java.util.List<Boolean> revisions = jdbcTemplate.query(
114:                "SELECT enabled FROM fix122_replay_revision WHERE test_run_id=?",
115:                (rs,row) -> rs.getBoolean(1), runId);
116:        replayScope.fix122Enabled(revisions == null || revisions.isEmpty() || revisions.get(0));
117:        for (TradeSignal signal : timeline) {
118:            // FIX-090: shadow execution is frequently cancellable without affecting Production.
119:            if (stopRequested != null && stopRequested.getAsBoolean()) {
120:                throw new ReplayCancellationException(runId);
121:            }
122:            // FIX-052 exact ordering: BinanceKlineService invokes live position protection
123:            // from the 1m price update BEFORE publishing the candle-close analysis event.
124:            // Consume all persisted Production prices up to this signal timestamp before
125:            // making the freshly generated signal visible to position management.
126:            while (livePriceIndex < livePrices.size()
127:                    && (stopRequested == null || !stopRequested.getAsBoolean())
128:                    && !livePrices.get(livePriceIndex).observedAt().isAfter(signal.getGeneratedAt())) {
129:                MarketPriceEventService.PriceEvent live = livePrices.get(livePriceIndex++);
130:                replayScope.referencePrice(symbol, live.observedAt(), live.price());
131:                // FIX-11Q: advance only the isolated counterfactual grace position. This never mutates
132:                // the baseline Shadow position/cash/trades and therefore cannot change Replay parity.
133:                oneCandleContinuationGraceReplayObserver.onPrice(runId, symbol, live, latest1m, latest5m, latest1h);
134:                if (open != null && !live.observedAt().isBefore(executionStart) && !live.observedAt().isAfter(executionEnd)) {
135:                    LivePriceEvaluation protection = evaluateLivePrice(runId, symbol, open, live, latest1m, latest5m, latest1h);
136:                    open = protection.position();
137:                    if (protection.partialProceeds().signum() > 0) {
138:                        cash = cash.add(protection.partialProceeds(), MC);
139:                        realized = realized.add(protection.partialRealizedPnl(), MC);
140:                    }
141:                    ExitDecision liveExit = protection.decision();
142:                    if (liveExit.exit()) {
143:                        BigDecimal proceeds = live.price().multiply(open.quantity(), MC);
144:                        BigDecimal pnl = proceeds.subtract(open.cost(), MC);
145:                        BigDecimal pnlPct = percentage(open.entryPrice(), live.price());
146:                        BigDecimal totalPositionPnl = open.partialRealizedPnl().add(pnl, MC);
147:                        BigDecimal totalPositionPnlPct = totalPositionPnlPercent(open, pnl);
148:                        cash = cash.add(proceeds, MC);
149:                        realized = realized.add(pnl, MC);
150:                        trades++;
151:                        if (totalPositionPnl.signum() > 0) wins++; else if (totalPositionPnl.signum() < 0) losses++;
152:                        // wallet_execution_test stores this terminal leg; wallet_position_test stores
153:                        // the whole lifecycle including any earlier FIX-11T partial realization.
154:                        persistSellAtPrice(runId, symbol, live, latest1m, open, liveExit, pnl, pnlPct);
155:                        closePositionAtPrice(runId, open.positionId(), live, liveExit, totalPositionPnl, totalPositionPnlPct);
156:                        replayScope.recordStop(symbol, open.positionId(), live.observedAt(), liveExit.reason());
157:                        executionIntelligenceService.completePositionOpportunity(symbol, latest1m, liveExit.reason());
158:                                                open = null;
159:                    }
160:                }

22: * Automatic, token-free repair of authoritative local candle evidence.  It is
23: * deliberately not an analysis or execution service: recovered candles obtain
24: * SKIPPED_NOT_LIVE outcome evidence and wait for a subsequent genuine live
25: * close to resume ordinary analysis.
26: */
27:@Service
28:public class LocalCandleReconciliationService {
29:    private static final org.slf4j.Logger log=org.slf4j.LoggerFactory.getLogger(LocalCandleReconciliationService.class);

40:    private volatile Instant lastReconnectAdmission;

42:    @org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired(required=false) private ExchangeClockSynchronizer exchangeClock;
43:    @Value("${shared-market.mode:OFF}") private String mode;
44:    @Value("${local-candle.reconciliation.enabled:false}") private boolean enabled;

62:        request("RECONNECT_"+generation);

64:    public boolean isIdle(){return !running.get();}
65:    public Map<String,Object> health(){
66:        Map<String,Object> result=new LinkedHashMap<>();result.put("enabled",enabled);result.put("inFlight",running.get());
67:        try {
68:            result.put("pendingCandidates",jdbc.queryForObject("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM local_reconciliation_audit WHERE status='PENDING'",Long.class));
69:            result.put("recoveredCount",jdbc.queryForObject("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM local_reconciliation_audit WHERE status='RECOVERED'",Long.class));
70:            result.put("gapBackfilledCount",jdbc.queryForObject("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM local_reconciliation_audit WHERE status='GAP_BACKFILLED'",Long.class));
71:            result.put("expiredCount",jdbc.queryForObject("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM local_reconciliation_audit WHERE status='EXPIRED'",Long.class));
72:            Instant local=Instant.now();Instant reference=exchangeClock==null?local:exchangeClock.correctJvm(local);
73:            List<Map<String,Object>> activeGaps=new ArrayList<>();
74:            for(String symbol:coins.enabledSymbols())for(String interval:properties.getIntervals()){
75:                Instant lower=reference.minusSeconds(Math.max(1,minimumCandles)*intervalSeconds(interval));
76:                Timestamp first=jdbc.queryForObject("SELECT MIN(open_time) FROM candle WHERE symbol=? AND interval_code=?",Timestamp.class,symbol,interval);
77:                if(first==null)continue;
78:                Long count=jdbc.queryForObject("""
79:                    SELECT COUNT(*) FROM local_reconciliation_audit
80:                    WHERE subject_type='CANDLE_DATA' AND symbol=? AND interval_code=?
81:                      AND status='EXPIRED' AND failure_reason='NOT_ON_EXCHANGE'
82:                      AND candle_open_time>=? AND candle_open_time>?
83:                    """,Long.class,symbol,interval,Timestamp.from(lower),first);
84:                if(count!=null&&count>0)activeGaps.add(Map.of("symbol",symbol,"interval",interval,"count",count));
85:            }
86:            result.put("exchangeGapBlockedByLane",activeGaps);
87:            result.put("exchangeGapBlockedCount",activeGaps.stream().mapToLong(row->((Number)row.get("count")).longValue()).sum());
88:        }catch(RuntimeException unavailable){result.put("status","NOT_MIGRATED_OR_UNAVAILABLE");}
89:        return result;
90:    }
